1	# Changelog
2	
3	All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.
4	
5	## [Unreleased]
6	
7	### Added
8	
9	- **Juice & atmosphere pass**: the world now reacts and breathes, all via procedural rendering/audio (zero new assets) with **no save-format or worldgen impact**
10	  - **Particles**: a pure, unit-tested structure-of-arrays particle pool (`lib/game/render/particlePool.ts`) wrapped by a single `THREE.Points` draw call (`particleSystem.ts`) whose attributes refill each frame; a small shader draws each particle as a soft round sprite that fades over its life (no texture). The renderer gained `handleEvent(event, state)`, wired into the event drain beside `audio.handleEvent`: breaking a block throws shards tinted from `BLOCK_COLORS`, placing puffs, mob death bursts in the mob's body color, eating drops crumbs, and jumping/landing kick dust (landing scales with impact). Footstep dust spawns from a stride-distance accumulator. The three positioned events (`blockBroken`/`blockPlaced`/`mobDied`) gained additive `x,y,z` fields so bursts land correctly
11	  - **Night sky**: a deterministic 800-point star field that fades in at dusk, sprite discs for the moon and a now-visible sun riding opposite ends of the sun arc, and a drifting cloud sheet from a seamlessly tiling canvas-noise mask (`starField.ts`, `skyView.ts`). All camera-following and fog-exempt; opacities ramp off `daylight`
12	  - **Weather**: a pure, deterministic system (`engine/systems/weather.ts`) sets a **transient** `state.weather` (never serialized) — time splits into windows, a seeded hash decides precipitation, and biome picks snow (mountains) / clear (desert, ocean) / rain. It drives a camera-following precipitation field (`precipitation.ts`), an overcast sky tint + dimmed light + nearer fog in `syncDayNight`, and a synthesized looping rain bed on the audio graph (`rainLoop.ts`). Strictly cosmetic — spawn/daylight balance is untouched. New `WEATHER_CYCLE_SECONDS` / `WEATHER_RAIN_FRACTION` tunables
13	- **UI polish pass for a more authentic Minecraft look**: the interface now reads closer to real Minecraft across five fronts
14	  - **Pixel font**: UI text switches from the generic monospace stack to **Monocraft**, an open-source Minecraft-style face (SIL OFL 1.1) self-hosted via `next/font/local` from a committed woff2 in `app/fonts/` and exposed as the `--mc-font` CSS variable. Its coding ligatures are disabled so UI text stays literal. The monospace stack stays as the fallback and is pinned on the F3 debug readout, which relies on column alignment. This bundled font file is a deliberate, documented exception to the zero-binary-asset rule (the only one)
15	  - **Textured GUI chrome**: panels, inventory slots, recipe entries, and buttons gain a faint generated grain instead of flat fills. A new `grayGrainTileUrl` generator in `lib/ui/chromeTiles.ts` installs `--mc-tile-panel/well/button` as CSS variables (sunken bias on the slot well); the CSS layers each tile over the existing flat color, so bevels are untouched and SSR/pre-install falls back to the solid color
16	  - **Item tooltips**: hovering hotbar, inventory, and recipe entries now shows a cursor-following, near-black tooltip with a violet gradient border (new `useItemTooltip` hook in `components/game/ItemTooltip.tsx`) instead of the browser's native `title=`. Durable items show a gray "Durability x / y" line and locked recipes show "Requires <station>". The tooltip is `pointer-events:none` and `aria-hidden`, so it never blocks the pointer-lock click and accessible names (the `aria-label`s) are unchanged
17	  - **Depth**: a shared `.menu-backdrop` dims the frozen world behind the inventory and pause menu (the dimming moved off `.pause-overlay` so it no longer double-darkens), plus an always-on screen-edge `.vignette` over the 3D view (tunable via `--mc-vignette`)
18	  - **Motion**: a centered pop-in for the inventory panel, a fade-in for the pause overlay, quick hover/press feedback on slots and buttons, and a small rise on the hotbar item-name pop — all disabled under `prefers-reduced-motion`
19	  - No save-format or worldgen impact
20	- **Pickup & status toasts**: because mob loot drops straight into inventory storage (no ground item), kills could feel like they paid out nothing. A kill now shows a brief on-screen toast just above the hotbar (e.g. "+2 Wool, +1 Raw Mutton"). The same in-game toast also surfaces the sleep-denied messages ("You can only sleep at night" / "Monsters are nearby"), which previously only rendered inside the pause menu and so were invisible during play
21	- **Animal breeding**: right-click a sheep or horse with wheat (or a chicken with seeds) to put it "in love"; two in-love adults of the same kind standing close together spawn a baby that follows the parents, can't be farmed for drops, and grows to full size after ~90 seconds. This makes mob drops renewable, closing the loop back to combat loot. The passive population is capped (24) and feeding costs crops, so it stays bounded
22	  - New `MobState.fedTimer`/`ageTimer`, a `breeding` system ticked after mob AI, and a shared `findAimedMobIndex` so feeding and attacking use the same "what's in my crosshair" rule. Feeding joins the right-click precedence ahead of block interaction. New fed/bred sounds; babies render at 55% scale. Breeding state is session-only (mobs are never saved). No save-format or worldgen impact
23	- **Furnace & cooking**: craft a furnace (8 cobble) and right-click it to open the crafting panel in furnace mode, which unlocks smelting recipes — raw chicken or mutton + 1 planks (the fuel) cook into the cooked version, restoring 8 hunger versus 3 raw. Smelting recipes show as locked ("Requires Furnace") until a furnace is open
24	  - Reuses the Phase 2 interact system and the existing crafting panel — no separate furnace UI. Recipes gained an optional `station` field; the gate is enforced engine-side in the `craft` command (UI gating alone is spoofable). New `Furnace` block with a glowing-mouth atlas tile, `cooked_chicken`/`cooked_mutton` items, and a smelt sound. No save-format or worldgen impact
25	- **Farming & food**: craft a wood hoe (2 planks + 1 wood), right-click grass or dirt to till it into farmland, then right-click farmland with seeds to plant wheat. Crops grow through four stages over ~2.5 minutes and, when mature, harvest into wheat plus 1–2 seeds; an immature crop just returns its seed. Craft 3 wheat into bread (restores 6 hunger)
26	  - Seeds come from breaking grass (20% chance per block) — `addBlockDrop` now rolls a per-block `rollBlockDrops` table (`lib/game/items.ts`) instead of a single fixed drop
27	  - New **random-tick system** (`lib/game/engine/systems/randomTicks.ts`): each interval samples columns near the player and runs per-block handlers — the extensible basis for crop growth (and future saplings / grass spread). Crops are solid full-cube blocks (so they can be targeted and harvested) with each growth stage its own `BlockId`, which means they persist through the existing block-diff save with **no save-format change**
28	  - New `Farmland` + `WheatStage0..3` blocks, `wood_hoe`/`seeds`/`wheat`/`bread` items with generated sprites, and till/plant sounds. The right-click "use held item" step joins the interact precedence (after block interaction, before placement). No worldgen changes
29	- **Beds & sleeping**: craft a bed (3 wool + 3 planks), place it, and right-click it at night to skip to morning — the screen fades to black, the day clock jumps to a fresh dawn, and the bed becomes your respawn point (respawn falls back to a random land point if the bed is gone). Sleeping is refused during the day or with a hostile within 12 blocks, with an on-screen reason
30	  - New **right-click interact system** (`lib/game/engine/systems/interact.ts`): a fixed precedence in the `placeBlock` command runs block interaction before placement, so interactive blocks (beds now; furnaces later) take the click instead of getting a block placed on them. New `Bed` block + `bed` item, synthesized sleep/wake sounds, and a `SleepOverlay` fade component
31	  - **Save format v3** (additive — `SAVE_KEY` unchanged, v1/v2 saves still load): time of day (`dayClock`), `hearts`, `hunger`, and the bed `spawnPoint` now persist. Previously all four reset on reload, so sleeping through the night wouldn't have survived a reload. `readSave` chains the migrations v1 → v2 → v3
32	- **Mob loot & drops**: mobs now drop kind-specific items when they die instead of the old flat "cobble for hostiles, food for everything else" rule. Sheep drop wool + raw mutton, chickens feather + raw chicken, horses leather, zombies rotten flesh, skeletons bone, spiders string — counts are randomized per the engine's RNG (`lib/game/mobLoot.ts`). A new `mobDied` engine event plays a synthesized death thud
33	  - New items: `wool`, `feather`, `bone`, `leather`, `string` (crafting materials) and `rotten_flesh`, `raw_chicken`, `raw_mutton` (edible food), each with a generated 16×16 sprite (zero-asset)
34	  - **Per-food hunger**: food now restores a value carried on the item itself rather than one global constant. The generic `food` item still restores 7; raw meats restore 3, rotten flesh 2. New `food` and `material` item kinds; the legacy `food` item is reinterpreted (old saves load unchanged — saves store only item id + count)
35	  - New recipe: 4 string → 1 wool, so spider drops feed the wool supply (and, later, beds)
36	  - No save-format or worldgen impact
37	  - Selection recolors the body live (material color swap, no rebuild) and persists as a player preference under its own localStorage key (`minecraft_skin_v1`), separate from the world save — it survives world resets. No save-format or worldgen impact
38	- **Camera view toggle (V)**: cycles first-person → third-person rear → third-person front, like Minecraft's F5 (V instead, because F5 reloads the page in browsers)
39	  - New humanoid player body (head, torso, two arms, two legs) in the zero-asset box-mesh style, visible only in third person — walk gait scaled by speed, a chop animation on attacks and while mining, the look pitch on the head, and the held hotbar item rendered in the right hand via the shared item-model builder
40	  - The third-person camera boom raycasts against terrain and clamps so walls never occlude the player; the front view flips the heading and inverts the tilt while mouse control of the player stays unchanged
41	  - Gameplay is intentionally eye-relative in every mode (mining/placing reach, combat aim, audio panning are unaffected); the crosshair stays centered, matching Minecraft
42	  - The view mode is session-only (resets to first-person on reload). No save-format or worldgen impact
43	
44	### Documentation
45	
46	- **Added `docs/manual.md`**, a player-facing guide: getting started / your first day, the full control map, survival (health, hunger gates, combat, armor, death), the day-night thresholds, mining with tool-tier gating, crafting and smelting, the mob roster and breeding, farming, beds and sleeping, the procedural audio, and saving. Linked from the README and AGENTS.md
47	- **Added `docs/reference.md`**, scannable tables cross-checked against the code: all 31 recipes, the 26 block types (hardness + tool gate), the 6 mobs (stats + drop ranges), and item stats for tools, weapons, armor, and food
48	- **Added `docs/tuning.md`**, a contributor balance guide that groups the `config.ts` tunables by gameplay effect (player feel, survival pressure, danger, progression, farming/breeding, persistence/rendering), names the system that reads each, and flags the save-sensitive ones (`SAVE_KEY`, inventory layout)
49	- **Refreshed `docs/architecture.md`** to match the shipped engine: the per-frame step order now lists the random-tick (crop growth) and animal-breeding systems, the right-click precedence documents the full feed → interact → use-held → place chain, the mob/block drop tables (`rollMobDrops`/`rollBlockDrops`) are described, and the `window.__monecraft` debug handle the Playwright `e2e/` suite drives through is noted
50	
51	## [0.5.0] - 2026-06-13
52	
53	### Added
54	
55	- **Animated first-person held item**: a one-shot swing on attack clicks (hit or miss, via a new `attackSwung` engine event), a looping swing while mining, a walk bob scaled by movement speed, an equip dip when switching slots, and a faint idle sway
56	- Held tools, weapons, and food are now built by extruding their 16×16 inventory sprites into pixel-thick voxel meshes (one geometry, vertex colors), so the in-hand model always matches the icon; held blocks remain cubes
57	- Redesigned knife sprite: a single-edged drop-point blade with a bright cutting edge, dark spine, and riveted handle — clearly distinct from the swords (no crossguard). No save-format or worldgen impact
58	- **Procedural audio** — the game has sound, with zero audio assets (everything is synthesized at runtime, like the sprite system):
59	  - Block interaction SFX by material (stone/wood/grass/sand/glass/water): break, place, and staged mining hit ticks
60	  - Player feedback: surface-aware footsteps, jump and impact-scaled landing, hurt, eating, death/respawn stingers
61	  - Mob sounds: idle calls (sheep, chicken, horse, zombie, skeleton, spider) with distance falloff and look-relative stereo pan inside a 24-block earshot, plus attack and melee-hit sounds
62	  - Generative background music: a pentatonic ambient pad that brightens and quickens by day, darkens at night, and shifts with the biome (5 s hysteresis at borders); ducks behind the pause menu
63	  - Pause-menu Sound section: master and music sliders plus mute, persisted under a separate localStorage key (`minecraft_audio_v1`)
64	  - One-shot SFX ride on new engine `GameEvent`s (block broken/placed, hurt, ate, jumped, landed, mob attacked/hit); continuous sound derives from state, mirroring the renderer
65	  - New dependency: [zzfx](https://github.com/KilledByAPixel/ZzFX) (~1 KB, MIT) for SFX synthesis; the AudioContext is created only on the first user gesture (autoplay policy)
66	  - No save-format or worldgen impact
67	
68	## [0.4.0] - 2026-06-12
69	
70	### Added
71	
72	- Snow and Cactus blocks (mineable, placeable; cactus deals no contact damage). Snow caps mountain tops above y=68; cacti scatter across dry desert sand
73	- Sand beaches where low land meets the sea (shoreline band around sea level)
74	
75	- **Minecraft-style UI overhaul**: pixel-art hotbar with white selection outline and fading item-name popup, heart/hunger/armor icon rows, survival-layout inventory (armor column, 9×3 storage grid, hotbar row) with a visual recipe book (ingredient icons → result), pause menu (Esc) with Save/Load/Reset and a controls reference, red-tinted "You Died!" death screen with a Respawn button, and a toggleable F3 debug overlay (position, daylight, mob counts, FPS)
76	- Procedural pixel-art sprite system (`lib/ui/`): 16×16 item icons (isometric block cubes from `BLOCK_COLORS`, shape×material-palette tools/weapons/armor), HUD icons (hearts, drumsticks, armor), and UI noise tiles — all generated in code, no image assets, covered by integrity tests
77	- Top-right minimap rendered from world block data (north-up, height-shaded, player arrow, refreshes on block edits)
78	- Engine commands: `pause`/`resume` (freezes the whole simulation behind the menu), `toggleDebug`, and `respawn` (skips the death countdown)
79	
80	### Fixed
81	
82	- On slow machines the simulation ran in slow motion: the frame loop clamped each frame to one 50 ms step, so at low FPS game time fell behind wall time. The loop now catches up with bounded substeps
83	
84	### Changed
85	
86	- **Fewer, better-spread animals**: the day-one passive population drops from 34 to 14 (6 sheep, 5 chickens, 3 horses) and scatters over a wider ring than hostiles, so the spawn area no longer feels crowded. Initial hostiles and night spawning are unchanged. Mobs and the respawn point also no longer place on flooded columns
87	- **Worldgen rebalanced — `SAVE_KEY` bumped to `minecraft_save_v5`, existing saves are discarded.** The biome noise field was degenerate (whole maps collapsed to 1–2 biomes; forests effectively never generated, leaving some worlds nearly woodless). Maps now contain coherent patches of all five biomes (forest ~19–31%, measured across seeds). Tree canopies no longer overwrite trunk tops (trees were losing 2 wood blocks each and looked like bushes), tree density roughly doubled, and forests grow taller trunks. Worldgen hash tests re-baselined per the documented policy
88	- **Stats rebalanced to Minecraft ranges** — health 50 → 20 (10 hearts), energy renamed to hunger and rescaled 100 → 20 (10 drumsticks) with total drain ranges preserved (sprint 100 blocks / walk 300 / 50 jumps per point), food restores 7; health regen now requires hunger ≥ 12 and sprinting needs hunger > 6; hunger refills on respawn; fall/void damage rescaled to the new HP range
89	- **Hostile mobs hit harder**: zombie/skeleton damage 1 → 3, spider 1 → 2 (the old values were ~2% of max HP per hit; this is a real difficulty increase)
90	- **Inventory shrunk to 36 slots (9-slot hotbar + 27 storage)** to match the Minecraft layout; the `Digit0` hotbar binding is gone (1–9 only)
91	- **Save format bumped to version 2** (same `SAVE_KEY`, no worldgen impact): v1 saves are migrated on load — slots are compacted 40 → 36 with stackables merged, `selectedSlot` clamped to 0–8; items that genuinely overflow the smaller inventory are dropped
92	- Save/Load/Reset buttons moved from the top-left HUD (now removed) into the pause menu; the old top-left info lives in the F3 overlay
93	- Escape now opens the pause menu (pointer-lock loss during gameplay pauses the game; Esc also closes the inventory)
94	
95	## [0.3.0] - 2026-06-11
96	
97	### Added
98	
99	- Playwright E2E smoke suite (`bun run test:e2e`, also a CI job): boots the production build in headless Chromium and verifies rendering, input → movement, crafting via UI, mining, and save persistence through a `window.__monecraft` debug handle (also usable from the browser console)
100	- React component tests for InventoryPanel and Hotbar, and Three.js-only unit tests for mob visuals and the held item, all under `bun test` via a happy-dom preload
101	- Test suite (`bun test`): worldgen determinism hash tests (save-compat guard), meshing snapshots, raycast/collision/save round-trip/item-recipe integrity tests, pure inventory unit tests, and headless engine simulation tests (movement, energy, regen, mining, crafting, death/respawn, night spawning, save round-trips)
102	- Quality tooling: `bun run typecheck` (tsc), Prettier + `.editorconfig`, GitHub Actions CI (lint, typecheck, format, test, build on pinned Bun)
103	- WebGL failure handling: a fallback panel replaces a crash when the renderer cannot start
104	- `CONTRIBUTING.md`
105	
106	### Changed
107	
108	- **Engine rewrite**: gameplay simulation moved out of the 1,100-line React hook into a headless, framework-agnostic `GameEngine` (`lib/game/engine/`) with one system module per mechanic and a single `Command` entry point for all UI/input intents. Rendering is isolated in `GameRenderer` (`lib/game/render/`), DOM input in `lib/game/input/`, and the hook is now a thin shell using `useSyncExternalStore`. The mirrored refs-vs-state bridge and its ESLint rule disables are gone. No save-format or worldgen impact — verified by hash tests and save round-trip tests.
109	- Split `lib/world.ts` into `lib/world/` modules (blocks, voxelWorld, generation, meshing, atlas, queries) behind an index barrel; worldgen constants named in a frozen `GEN` object. No save-format or worldgen impact — output verified byte-identical by hash tests.
110	- Split `lib/game/config.ts` into `config.ts` (named tunables), `items.ts`, and `recipes.ts`; inventory slot math extracted to pure `lib/game/inventory.ts`
111	- Held-item block palette moved next to the atlas palette in `lib/world/blocks.ts` (same values)
112	- Per-frame `Vector3` allocations in mob AI, combat, and player motion replaced with module-scope scratch vectors
113	- Upgraded dependencies: Next.js 14.2 → 16.2 (Turbopack), React 18.3 → 19.2, Three.js r168 → r184, TypeScript 5.6 → 6.0, ESLint 8 → 10 with flat config (`next lint` was removed upstream; `bun run lint` now runs `eslint .`)
114	- Replaced `eslint-config-next` (blocked on ESLint 10 by `eslint-plugin-react`) with a hand-rolled flat config: `@eslint/js`, `typescript-eslint`, `@eslint-react/eslint-plugin`, `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` v7, `@next/eslint-plugin-next`
115	- Removed dead code in world generation flagged by the new lint stack (`seededHash`, useless biome-height initializers)
116	
117	### Fixed
118	
119	- Unhandled `requestPointerLock()` rejection (surfaced by the E2E suite): clicking any UI button triggered a lock attempt whose failure logged an uncaught error in some environments; the game now stays unlocked silently
120	- Crafting with a full inventory no longer consumes the cost while silently destroying the crafted result; the recipe is refused instead (the craft button was already disabled in this case, so this was only reachable programmatically)
121	
122	## [0.2.0] - 2026-06-11
123	
124	### Added
125	
126	- Survival systems: health regen, energy bar with distance/jump-based drain, food mechanics, hotbar hearts and health progress bar
127	- Day-night cycle with hostile mob behavior — night spawning, twilight-only spider aggression, daylight burn for zombies/skeletons
128	- Durability system for tools, weapons, and armor, persisted in saves
129	- Equipped armor slots with damage reduction
130	- Ore/gear progression: gold, sliver, ruby, sapphire, and diamond tiers with crafting recipes
131	- Biome-based terrain (oceans, forests, deserts, mountains) with caves, chambers, and tuned ore distribution
132	- Block breaking crack overlay (8-stage, hardness-scaled)
133	- Save system with block-diff persistence, autosave, and Reset World button
134	- Inventory expansion: 99-stack system, hotbar slot swapping, held item view, scrollable crafting list
135	- Emergency unstuck (auto safeguard + `U` key)
136	- Shared 16×16 per-face block texture atlas with multi-layer terrain shading and ambient occlusion
137	
138	### Changed
139	
140	- World deepened to 150 blocks with wider cave generation
141	- Water rendered from both sides; players can stay submerged without unstuck teleport
142	- Game runtime refactored into modular systems (`lib/game/runtime/`) with split UI/styles
143	- Smooth terrain noise (fixes tall column artifacts); Plains-biome spawn priority
144	- Mob combat tightened: no hitting through blocks, vertical melee reach check, knockback
145	
146	### Fixed
147	
148	- Map persistence and player-stuck bugs (collision depenetration, load-time relocation safety)
149	- Startup freeze from world generation load
150	- Dark block undersides via brighter tiles and bounce lighting
151	
152	## [0.1.0]
153	
154	Initial Minecraft-like prototype: procedural voxel terrain, first-person movement with gravity and collisions, block breaking/placing, hotbar, and basic mobs. Built with Next.js, TypeScript, Three.js, and Bun.

10	After shipping two passes on `main` (an authentic-Minecraft UI pass and a juice & atmosphere pass — particles, night sky, transient weather; both in CHANGELOG), these are the highest-value gaps and the candidate directions I proposed. The user picked "juice & atmosphere" first; the rest are **not yet chosen or built**:
11	
12	- **Caves & survival tension** — torches + a real block-light propagation system (caves are currently lit like the surface, no darkness), plus lava + lava/drowning damage + an oxygen meter. Largest systems work (touches the mesher).
13	- **Exploration & loot** — chests for persistent storage (a core gap; would touch the save format), plus dungeons/loot structures with chests and a mob spawner. Touches worldgen + save.
14	- **Endgame & ranged combat** — bows/arrows (a projectile system; skeletons could shoot back) and a boss encounter that turns the diamond-tier grind into an actual goal.
15	
16	Other conspicuous gaps noted during exploration: no block light, no storage/chests, no exploration payoff/endgame past mining diamond, no fluid hazard, no ranged combat. The game already has caves, ores, ~120 houses, 5 (cosmetic) biomes, static water, 6 mobs, farming/breeding/cooking/beds, and 7-tier gear. See [[review-fixes-no-push]] and [[no-pr-attribution]] for the user's workflow preferences.

## Feasibility Assessment: Block-Light Propagation System

I've thoroughly explored the rendering and meshing pipeline. Here's what I found:

### 1. THE MESHER: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/meshing.ts` (lines 71-179)

**Current lighting approach:**
- **Per-face direction-based shading only**: Lines 98-101 define `materialTint()` which applies a simple face-normal multiplier (1.0 for top, 0.95 for bottom, 0.9 for sides)
- **Ambient Occlusion (AO) only light source**: Lines 117-135 compute `faceOcclusion()` which samples 4 neighboring voxels around each face to calculate darkening (max 0.8 minimum brightness per line 134)
- **Vertex colors only**: Colors are baked per-vertex and stored as float RGB in the geometry (line 175). The only per-vertex light influence is the AO multiplier applied to base material tints
- **No per-voxel light values exist**: The mesh building reads only block type and face direction; there's no light propagation input

**Entry point for meshing:**
- `buildGeometryRegion(world, minX, maxX, minZ, maxZ, minY, maxY)` at line 71
- Called from GameRenderer at `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/GameRenderer.ts` line 334 in `rebuildWorldMesh()`
- The material is `THREE.MeshStandardMaterial` with `vertexColors: true` (line 87), `roughness: 0.88`, `metalness: 0.02`

**Technical challenge for per-block lighting:**
The mesher is straightforward but would need significant restructuring:
- Currently iterates over blocks (line 137-170), checks face visibility, and emits vertices with pre-computed colors
- To add per-block light: would need to sample light level at each corner of each face, interpolate across the quad, and modulate the AO+tint calculation
- The light level lookup path would add ~5-10 dependent reads per vertex, and light propagation computation happens at world edit time (see below)

---

### 2. DAY-NIGHT LIGHTING: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/dayNight.ts` (lines 1-25)

**Current implementation is purely global:**
- `daylightAt(dayClock)` returns a single scalar 0.04–1.0 (line 5-8)
- `sunAngleAt(dayClock)` returns sun rotation in radians (lines 10-13)
- **No per-location darkness**: The entire world is lit identically by time of day

**How it drives rendering:**
In `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/GameRenderer.ts`, `syncDayNight()` (lines 341-361):
- Updates `sun.position` based on `sunAngleAt()` (line 345)
- Modulates `sun.intensity = 0.2 + daylight * 1.2` (line 346)
- Modulates `hemiLight.intensity = 0.24 + daylight * 1.05` (line 347)
- Interpolates sky color `nightSky` ↔ `daySky` by daylight factor (line 349)
- Weather system overlays overcast dimming (lines 353-358)

**Caves are identically lit as the surface:** The scene uses a global directional light (sun) + hemisphere light. Cave interiors receive zero sunlight physically but are lit by the full hemisphere ambient regardless of depth. There's no darkness simulation—caves are bright as surface when it's day, dim as surface when it's night. This is the core motivation for the feature.

---

### 3. VOXEL WORLD DATA STRUCTURE: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/voxelWorld.ts` (lines 1-73)

**Current storage is single-byte per voxel:**
```typescript
readonly blocks: Uint8Array;  // line 16
```
- Addressed via `index(x, y, z) = x + z * sizeX + y * sizeX * sizeZ` (lines 26-28)
- World size is 512×150×512 = 39.3 million voxels (line 1 defines `WORLD_SIZE_X = 512`, `WORLD_SIZE_Y = 150`, `WORLD_SIZE_Z = 512`)
- **BlockId enum** at `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/blocks.ts` (lines 5-34) uses values 0–26 (33 block types), leaving room in Uint8Array

**Storage impact for light levels:**
- Adding a light channel would require either:
  - **Dual Uint8Array** (one for block ID, one for light level) — adds 39.3 MB overhead, cache-unfriendly
  - **Packing into a single Uint8Array** (4 bits block type + 4 bits light) — breaks the save format, per line 8-9 comment: "saves address voxels through index()... changing either breaks every existing save"
  - **Sparse light map** (hash map of lit voxels) — minimal overhead, but requires per-lookup cost during mesh building and propagation

**Save format constraint:** The change is irreversible; saves embed block values at specific indices. Any structural change breaks backward compatibility.

---

### 4. BLOCK EDITS → RE-MESH TRIGGER PATH

The flow is well-instrumented:

**Place block** (`/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/interact.ts`, lines 96-125):
- Line 116: `state.blockChanges.set(tx, ty, tz, slot.blockId)` writes to the world
- Line 123: `state.worldMeshDirty = true` flags the mesh for rebuild

**Break block** (`/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/mining.ts`, lines 48-94):
- Line 88: `state.blockChanges.set(bx, by, bz, BlockId.Air)` breaks the block
- Line 91: `state.worldMeshDirty = true` flags the mesh

**Other edits** (farming, random ticks in `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/randomTicks.ts` line 20):
- All modify world via `blockChanges.set()` and set `worldMeshDirty = true`

**BlockChangeTracker** (`/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/blockChanges.ts`, lines 1-49):
- Tracks deltas from generated baseline for save persistence
- The `set()` method updates both world and the tracker

**Mesh rebuild** (`GameRenderer.ts`, lines 321-339):
- Line 324: If `worldMeshDirty` or player crosses `RENDER_GRID` boundary (20 blocks), rebuild is triggered
- Line 334: `buildGeometryRegion()` is called with a 180-block radius render window around the player
- Line 326: `worldMeshDirty` is cleared after rebuild

**Key observation:** There's no per-chunk dirty tracking. The entire visible region is rebuilt as a single monolithic mesh every time. This is fine for geometry changes (faces), but light propagation would be a separate concern.

---

### 5. EXISTING EMISSIVE / GLOW CONCEPT

**Furnace has visual glow in the texture only:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/atlas.ts`, line 79: Furnace side face has a special orange tone `[0.95, 0.45, 0.12]` applied to the mouth region to *look* glowing, but it doesn't emit light
- This is purely texture-space visual. No Three.js emissive material property is used for blocks

**Mobs have emissive eyes:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/mobModel.ts`, line 18: Eye material uses `emissive: new THREE.Color(eyeColor).multiplyScalar(0.15)`
- This is a small-scale Three.js emissive property, but blocks never use it

**No block emissive materials:** The world mesh uses a single `MeshStandardMaterial` (GameRenderer.ts line 85) with `vertexColors: true` but no per-material emissive map. All blocks share the same material.

---

### EFFORT ESTIMATE: LARGE (highest of the three candidates)

**Why this is complex:**

1. **Light propagation algorithm** (BFS/Dijkstra from light sources, handling 2 light types: daylight + emissive):
   - ~500–1000 LoC per light engine iteration
   - Must run on block edit (not per-frame)
   - Needs to propagate decay: light level 0–15, decays by 1 per block distance
   - Directional daylight requires height-based falloff calculation per column
   - Test coverage would be extensive (light reaching caves, blocking by solids, decay curves)

2. **Storage redesign** (light map architecture):
   - If dual Uint8Array: straightforward but wasteful (39 MB overhead)
   - If sparse hash map: cleaner but adds lookup cost in mesher hot path
   - If packed bits: saves format breakage, risky given "every existing save breaks" invariant
   - Chunk-based light caching would require rearchitecting world storage (currently monolithic 512×150×512)

3. **Mesher integration** (per-vertex light sampling & interpolation):
   - Currently: simple face tint × AO per vertex → 9 colors per face
   - With light: sample light at each face corner, interpolate, apply falloff, modulate vertex color
   - Adds ~4 light lookups per vertex in a hot loop (~50k+ vertices per render region)
   - Risk: performance regression if not optimized

4. **Rendering changes:**
   - Material per-block or per-chunk instead of global (to support varied emissive maps)
   - Or: use a light map texture (requires UV repacking, runtime texture generation per mesh rebuild)
   - Fog adjustment for dark caves (currently fog is global, would need depth-based or light-based tweaking)

5. **Data flow changes:**
   - Block edits must trigger light propagation (new system)
   - Light propagation must mark affected mesh regions dirty (new dependency)
   - Config tuning: light source strengths, decay rates, cave darkness target
   - Testing: caves must render dark at night, torches must be visible underground, light must propagate realistically

**Line-count rough estimate:**
- Light propagation algorithm: 600–800 LoC
- Storage/architecture changes: 300–400 LoC
- Mesher light sampling: 200–300 LoC
- State & config: 100–150 LoC
- Tests & integration: 300–500 LoC
- **Total: ~1500–2200 LoC**, plus rearchitecting world storage or adding 39 MB overhead

**Timeline estimate:** 3–4 weeks for a full implementation with testing, or 1.5–2 weeks for a minimal "light map overlay" prototype (sparse, no storage redesign, texture-based rendering).

---

### SUMMARY

| Component | Finding | Complexity |
|-----------|---------|-----------|
| **Mesher** | Simple face tint + AO; no per-voxel light input | Adding light sampling is straightforward but adds per-vertex cost |
| **Day-night lighting** | Global directional + hemisphere only; no per-location darkness | Requires new light propagation engine |
| **Cave darkness** | Confirmed: caves are identically lit as surface (zero underground darkness today) | This is the pain point the feature solves |
| **Block storage** | Single Uint8Array per voxel; light channel breaks save format | Dual array or sparse map both viable but with trade-offs |
| **Edit→re-mesh** | Clean `worldMeshDirty` flag; full region rebuild every edit | Light propagation must be decoupled from mesh rebuild timing |
| **Emissive** | Furnace has visual glow (texture only); no block emissive today | Requires material or map changes to support emissive blocks |

**Feasibility: Moderate-to-high difficulty.** The architecture is sound and supports the feature, but it's a multi-week effort touching the world storage model, light propagation, and rendering pipeline. The save-format invariant is the main architectural constraint—any light storage redesign must be carefully planned to avoid breaking existing saves or incurring large overhead.

## Chest & Dungeon Feasibility Assessment Report

Based on a thorough exploration of the monecraft codebase, here's a detailed analysis of what chests and dungeons would need to touch:

### 1. Save Format & Block Metadata

**Files:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/docs/save-format.md` (save format spec)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/save.ts` (lines 1-72: read/write & migrations)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/types.ts` (lines 58-84: SaveData types)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/blockChanges.ts` (lines 1-49)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts` (lines 317-336: serialize())
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/config.ts` (line 88: `SAVE_KEY = "minecraft_save_v5"`)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/voxelWorld.ts` (lines 26-28: voxel index formula)

**Current state:**
- Save **version 3** (latest)
- Block storage is a **flat `Uint8Array` with voxel index: `x + z*sizeX + y*sizeX*sizeZ`**
- Block **diff** storage: `changes: [voxelIndex, blockId][]` (lines 8-9 in save-format.md, line 322 in GameEngine.ts)
- Saves store only diffs against the generated baseline; reversion to original drops the edit from the diff
- Migration chain: **v1→v2→v3** (`migrateSaveV1toV2` at line 16, `migrateSaveV2toV3` at line 50 in save.ts; `readSave` chains them at lines 55-68)

**Critical constraint:** **No existing per-block metadata system.** Currently, every block is just a `BlockId` (Uint8 enum). Bed and Furnace are single BlockIds with no attached data (no orientation, no contents).

**Chests feasibility challenge:** The block-diff format stores only `[index, blockId]`. To add per-block container contents, you'd need to:
1. Extend `SaveData` with a new field (e.g., `blockEntities?: { [voxelIndex]: ChestContents }`) 
2. Bump the save version to 4 and migrate v3→v4
3. Update `serialize()` at line 317 and the restore path
4. Define `ChestContents` as an `InventorySlot[]`-like structure

The voxel index formula **must not change** (marked as save-format-critical in voxelWorld.ts line 9).

---

### 2. Inventory Model & Slot Structure

**Files:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/inventory.ts` (entire file: slot algebra)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/types.ts` (lines 23-37: InventorySlot type)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/config.ts` (lines 28-29: HOTBAR_SLOTS=9, INVENTORY_SLOTS=36)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx` (lines 1-150+: UI rendering)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/save.ts` (lines 74-123: `restoreInventorySlots`)

**Inventory design:**
```typescript
// from types.ts lines 23-37
type InventorySlot = {
  id: string | null;        // item ID or null
  label: string;            // display name
  kind: ItemKind | null;    // "block"|"weapon"|"tool"|"armor"|"food"|"material"
  count: number;            // stack size
  blockId?: BlockId;        // if it's a block item
  attack?: number;          // if weapon
  minePower?: number;       // if tool
  mineTier?: number;        // if tool
  armorSlot?: ArmorSlot;    // if armor
  defense?: number;         // if armor
  durability?: number;      // current durability
  maxDurability?: number;   // max durability
  hunger?: number;          // if food
};
```

**Pure slot algebra:** Every function returns `InventorySlot[] | null` (new array on change, null on "no change"). Functions like `adjustSlotCount`, `craft`, `consumeToolDurability` are immutable and re-usable.

**Chest contents reuse:** ✅ **Yes, identical slot structure can be reused.** A chest would hold `InventorySlot[]` (e.g., 27 slots, double-chest 54 slots). The same mutation functions (`adjustSlotCount`, `swapSlots`, etc.) work on any array.

### 3. Right-Click Interact System

**Files:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/interact.ts` (entire file)

2. `tryInteractBlock` (line 254) — open furnace / sleep in bed
3. `tryUseHeldItem` (line 255) — use hoe / plant seeds

**Current interactive blocks (interact.ts lines 14-19):**

**How it works:**

- Checks `INTERACTIVE_BLOCKS` map

**Furnace handler (lines 72-78):**

  state.inventoryOpen = true;
  state.craftingStation = "furnace";
  emit({ type: "openedStation", station: "furnace" });
  return true;
}
```

**Chests integration:** Add `ChestId` to enum, add to `INTERACTIVE_BLOCKS`, add `interactChest` handler that sets `state.containerOpen = true` with `state.openChestBlockIndex = voxelIndex` to track which chest is open.

### 4. Furnace UI Open Flow (Container UI Pattern)

**Files:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/state.ts` (lines 93-95: `inventoryOpen` & `craftingStation`)

**State flow:**

2. **Serialize:** not persisted (only `inventoryOpen` & `selectedSlot` are transient)

4. **React hook:** `useMinecraftGame()` maps engine commands to React via `useSyncExternalStore`

**On close:** When `inventoryOpen` becomes false, `craftingStation` is cleared (line 214, GameEngine.ts: `if (!state.inventoryOpen) state.craftingStation = null`)

**Chest pattern:** Would need:

### 5. Worldgen Structure Placement

**Files:**
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/generation.ts` (entire file)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/generation.test.ts` (lines 34-79: determinism tests)
- `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/docs/testing.md` (re-baseline policy)

**Structure generation flow (generation.ts lines 63-82):**
```typescript
export function generateWorld(world: VoxelWorld): void {
  // ...
  generateTerrain(world);
  carveCaves(world, rand);
  placeWater(world);
  placeBeaches(world);
  placeOres(world, rand);
  placeTrees(world, rand);
  placeCacti(world);
  placeStructures(world, rand);  // ← Line 81: this is the structure hook
}
```

**Current structure placement (lines 320-369):**
- `placeStructures` loops through 120 house attempts (`GEN.houseCount`, line 38)
- Per house: find XZ coords, call `placeHouse(world, cx, cz)`
- `placeHouse` checks terrain flatness, builds a 7×7 cobblestone/brick/wood building

**Determinism constraints (generation.test.ts lines 8-15, save-format.md lines 32-35):**
- **Must use the seeded `rand` PRNG** (lines 64-72 in generation.ts) — not `Math.random()`
- **Output is pinned by SHA-256 hashes** (line 40 in generation.test.ts)
- **Changing constants or order breaks all existing saves** — must bump `SAVE_KEY` if modifying generation
- **Full-world hash test** (lines 43-49): 512×150×512 world for seed 1337 → `"788cb3a2…"` must remain byte-identical
- **Re-baseline policy** (documented in testing.md, referenced line 34): only legitimate on deliberate CHANGELOG-flagged worldgen change or Bun/JSC version bump (Math.sin results are engine-defined)

**Dungeon placement pattern:**
```typescript
function placeStructures(world: VoxelWorld, rand: () => number): void {
  for (let i = 0; i < GEN.houseCount; i += 1) {
    // existing houses
    const cx = 12 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeX - 24));
    const cz = 12 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeZ - 24));
    placeHouse(world, cx, cz);
  }
  
  // NEW: dungeons with configurable count + density
  const dungeonConfig = { count: 60, chestChance: 0.3 };
  for (let i = 0; i < dungeonConfig.count; i += 1) {
    const cx = 12 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeX - 24));
    const cz = 12 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeZ - 24));
    placeDungeon(world, cx, cz, dungeonConfig.chestChance, rand);
  }
}
```

**Dungeon chest loot:** Would use the same `rollMobDrops` pattern (from `lib/game/mobLoot.ts`), injectable `rng`.

### Summary Table

| Concern | Current State | Chest Impact | Dungeon Impact |
|---------|---------------|--------------|-----------------|
| **Save format** | v3: block diffs only | ✅ Need v4 with `blockEntities: {index: ChestContents}[]`; migrate v3→v4 | 🔄 Loot items stored in chest entities |
| **Block storage** | Uint8Array, no metadata | ⚠️ **No per-block metadata system** — need new `blockEntities` parallel store | ⚠️ Same: dungeons are blocks + chest blockEntities |
| **Inventory model** | 36-slot arrays, immutable algebra | ✅ **Reusable:** chest is `InventorySlot[]`, same `adjustSlotCount` etc. | ✅ Same for chest loot |
| **Interact precedence** | Fixed: feed → block → item → place | ✅ Easy: add `Chest` to `INTERACTIVE_BLOCKS`, add `interactChest` handler | ✅ Chests are blocks in dungeons |
| **Container UI flow** | Furnace: `inventoryOpen + craftingStation` | ✅ Pattern works; extend with `containerOpen + openContainerBlockIndex` | ✅ Chests reuse container pattern |
| **Worldgen** | `placeStructures` loop with houses, RNG pinned by hash tests | 🟡 Hook exists; add dungeon pass with new `GEN.dungeonCount` constant — **bumps world hash, breaks saves** | **Requires v3→v4 migration + worldgen hash re-baseline** |

---

### Feasibility Verdict

**Chests: FEASIBLE — Medium Effort**
- Slot reuse ✅
- Interact hook ✅
- UI pattern ✅
- **Blocker:** No block metadata system → must add `blockEntities` to SaveData and bump version to 4

**Dungeons: FEASIBLE — Medium-to-High Effort**
- Structure placement hook exists ✅
- Determinism preserved (seeded RNG) ✅
- Loot gen exists (mobDrops) ✅
- **Blocker:** Worldgen change breaks all existing saves → **requires `SAVE_KEY` bump and v3→v4 migration**

**Implementation order:**
1. Add `blockEntities: { [index: number]: InventorySlot[] }` to `SaveData` and bump to v3.5 or v4
2. Extend `blockChanges.ts` to track block entity changes
3. Add `Chest` BlockId, UI panel, and interact handler
4. Add dungeon gen config to `GEN`, implement `placeDungeon()`, add chest placement with loot
5. Bump `SAVE_KEY` and add `migrateSaveV3toV4`
6. **Re-baseline** generation.test.ts hashes (expected and documented in PRs)

Perfect! Now I have comprehensive coverage. Let me compile my findings:

## Summary Report: Ranged Combat & Boss Feasibility Assessment

Based on a thorough exploration of the codebase, here are my findings across all requested systems:

---

### 1. COMBAT SYSTEM
**File: `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/combat.ts` (67 lines)**

**How melee attacks work:**
- **`findAimedMobIndex(state)`** (lines 23-44): Casts a ray from player eye (EYE_HEIGHT=1.62) along the look direction, finds mobs within ATTACK_REACH (4.5 units) that are within an aim cone (ATTACK_AIM_DOT=0.89, ~27° accuracy requirement). Returns the closest targeted mob index or -1.
- **`tryAttackMob(state, damage, onMobKilled)`** (lines 50-67): Applies damage directly to mob.hp (line 55), applies knockback (0.75 units horizontal + 0.12 vertical, line 61), calls `onMobKilled` callback when hp ≤ 0.
- **`weaponDamage(state)`** (lines 12-16): Returns weapon's `attack` value from inventory, defaults to FIST_DAMAGE (6).

**Damage application:** Direct HP subtraction, no damage drop-off, no armor system for mobs.
**Attack triggering:** Via `engine.dispatch({ type: "attack" })` in GameEngine.ts line 259-268; emits `attackSwung` event, triggers durability loss on hit.

**Key parameters (lib/game/config.ts):**
- ATTACK_REACH = 4.5
- FIST_DAMAGE = 6
- ATTACK_AIM_DOT = 0.89 (cosine of aim tolerance)
- EYE_HEIGHT = 1.62

---

### 2. MOB SYSTEM
**Files:**
- **MobState definition:** `lib/game/engine/state.ts` lines 26-49
- **Mob AI/ticking:** `lib/game/engine/systems/mobAI.ts` (102 lines)
- **Mob templates & spawning:** `lib/game/mobs.ts` (78 lines) + `lib/game/engine/systems/spawnDirector.ts` (94 lines)

**MobState structure (26-49 in state.ts):**
```
{
  id: number;           // Unique per-session ID (nextMobId increments)
  kind: MobKind;        // "zombie" | "skeleton" | "spider" | "sheep" | "chicken" | "horse"
  hostile: boolean;
  hp: number;           // Current health
  position: THREE.Vector3;  // Body center (ground + halfHeight)
  direction: THREE.Vector3; // Normalized movement direction
  yaw: number;          // Rotation around Y
  speed: number;        // Base movement speed (from template)
  moveSpeed: number;    // Runtime speed (after aggro/flee multipliers)
  detectRange: number;  // Aggro range
  attackDamage: number; // Melee hit damage to player
  attackCooldown: number; // Seconds between attacks
  attackTimer: number;  // Countdown to next attack
  halfHeight: number;   // For positioning (head center = position + halfHeight*0.35)
  bobSeed: number;      // Per-mob animation offset
  fedTimer: number;     // Breeding countdown (animals only)
  ageTimer: number;     // Baby countdown; > 0 = scaled 0.6x, drops nothing
}
```

**Mob spawning & removal:**
- **Spawning (spawnDirector.ts 17-45):** `spawnMobGroup()` creates MobState objects, pushes to `state.mobs[]`, increments `state.nextMobId`.
- **Removal (GameEngine.ts 369-381):** `removeMobAt(index)` splices from array, rolls loot via `rollMobDrops()`, emits `mobDied` event + `pickedUp` event. Babies (ageTimer > 0) drop nothing.

**Mob AI (mobAI.ts 23-102):**
- Per-tick loop: wander/aggro/flee logic, line-of-sight raycast for attack validation, attack cooldown countdown, daylight burn for zombies/skeletons.
- Hostiles aggro within detectRange, flee if player within 4.2 range and not hostile.
- Attack condition (lines 81): activeHostile + attackDistance < 4 + verticalGap < 1.6 + hasLineOfSight + attackTimer ≤ 0 → emit `mobAttacked`, apply damage to player via `applyDamage()`, apply knockback to player velocity.

**No projectile/entity concept exists.** Mobs are static in the `state.mobs[]` array; all queries iterate over mobs linearly. No entity ID system for non-mob objects.

**Skeleton behavior (mobs.ts 46-52):**
```
skeleton: {
  speed: 1.08, hp: 9, detectRange: 12,
  attackDamage: 3, attackCooldown: 1.4,
  modelArgs: [colors...scaled to 0.75×1.08×0.48]
}
```
Currently **melee only**—bones are visual details, no ranged weapons defined.

---

### 3. ITEMS & RECIPES
**Files:**
- **Item definitions:** `lib/game/items.ts` (194 lines)
- **Recipes:** `lib/game/recipes.ts` (245 lines)

**ItemKind enum (types.ts line 4):**
```typescript
export type ItemKind = "block" | "weapon" | "tool" | "armor" | "food" | "material";
```

**Current weapons (items.ts 98-105):** All are melee swords/knives with `attack: number` property (e.g., diamond_sword: attack=47, maxDurability=720). Weapon stats in InventorySlot:
```typescript
{ kind: "weapon", attack?: number, maxDurability?, durability? }
```

**Projectile requirements:**
- Would need a new ItemKind: `"ranged"` or split weapons into `"melee"` | `"ranged"`.
- Bow definition: `{ kind: "ranged", attack?: number, ammoType: "arrow", maxDurability }`.
- Arrow as material: `{ kind: "material" }` (not a consumable like food—arrows are retrieved from kills).

**No durability/ammo tracking on consumption.** Current system consumes tool durability once per successful action; arrows would need per-shot depletion or a separate ammo slot/count system.

**Example new recipes needed:**
```
"bow": "2 String + 3 Wood -> Bow"
"arrow": "1 Feather + 1 Flint + 1 Wood -> Arrow"
```

---

### 4. MOB RENDERING
**Files:**
- **Mob visuals sync:** `lib/game/render/mobVisuals.ts` (62 lines)
- **Mob model builder:** `lib/game/mobModel.ts` (72 lines)
- **Renderer event handling:** `lib/game/render/GameRenderer.ts` lines 149-245

**Current mob rendering (mobVisuals.ts 26-55):**
- Maps mob id → Three.js model.
- Per-frame: updates model position/rotation from MobState, applies bob animation (sine wave), applies leg gait animation (scaled by moveSpeed).
- On mob removal: disposes materials & geometries.

**Mob model structure (mobModel.ts):** BoxGeometry primitives (body, head, eyes, snout, legs) colored by material. Scalable via modelArgs (size tuples). Baby scaling via BABY_SCALE = 0.6.

**Rendering flying arrows:**
- Would need to track arrow state in engine (position, velocity, age, etc.)—separate from `state.mobs[]` or as a new `state.projectiles[]` array.
- mobVisuals.ts creates/destroys models on demand; an arrow renderer would follow the same pattern but animate differently (no gait, just linear motion + rotation).
- Particle system already exists for visual effects (particleSystem.ts); arrow impacts could emit bursts.

**Boss rendering considerations:**
- Could reuse mob model builder (scaled-up BoxGeometry) or create a specialized `createBossModel()`.
- No rigged animation system—all movement is transform-based, so a larger model with more body parts is straightforward.
- Mob visuals maps id → model, so a boss is just another mob with a unique kind and larger scale.

---

### 5. ENGINE EVENTS
**File:** `lib/game/engine/state.ts` lines 184-207

**Current GameEvent types:**
```typescript
"died" | "respawned"
"blockBroken" | "blockPlaced"
"playerHurt" | "ateFood" | "jumped" | "landed"
"mobAttacked" | "mobHit" | "mobDied"
"attackSwung"
"sleepStarted" | "sleepDenied" | "wokeUp"
"tilledSoil" | "plantedSeed"
"openedStation" | "smelted"
"mobFed" | "mobBred"
"pickedUp"
```

**Event flow:**
- Engine emits to `this.events: GameEvent[]` (GameEngine.ts line 68).
- Shell drains via `engine.consumeEvents()` (line 346).
- Shell passes events to `renderer.handleEvent(event, state)` and `audio.handleEvent(event)` (useMinecraftGame.ts line 20).
- Renderer spawns particles; audio plays SFX.

**Projectile events needed:**
- `"arrowFired"`: position, direction, shooter (for audio)
- `"arrowHit"`: hitType ("mob"|"block"), target, damageDealt (for particles/audio)
- `"projectileSpawned"` / `"projectileDespawned"`: optional, for cleanup audio

---

### 6. SYSTEM STEP ORDER
**File:** `docs/architecture.md` lines 38-53 + GameEngine.ts lines 151-200

**Per-frame execution order in engine.step():**
```
1. Pause gate: if paused, return early
2. Stuck detection / auto-unstuck (STUCK_RESET_SECONDS = 60 s)
3. Death check + respawn countdown (only mobs tick while dead)
4. Sleep gate: fade + clock skip
5. Player motion & physics (playerMotion.ts)
6. Hunger drain + health regen (playerStats.ts)
7. Mining progress & block breaking (mining.ts)
8. Day-night clock (dayNight.ts) + weather (weather.ts)
9. Random block ticks (randomTicks.ts)
10. Hostile spawning director (spawnDirector.ts)
11. Mob AI: wander/aggro/flee/attack (mobAI.ts)
12. Animal breeding (breeding.ts)
```

**Combat runs on command, not per-frame** (GameEngine.ts 259-268).

**Projectile system placement:**
- **Option A (before mob AI):** Insert `tickProjectiles()` at step 10.5, so arrows are updated before mobs can be hit, but after world queries are refreshed.
- **Option B (after mob AI):** Insert at step 12.5, to apply arrow damage after all mob movement is finalized. Risk: visual desync if arrow and mob both move in the same frame.
- **Recommended: Option A** — update projectiles before mobs, so hit detection is consistent with mob positions.

---

### 7. ARCHITECTURE CONSTRAINTS & IMPLICATIONS

**Hard boundaries that affect projectiles:**

1. **Mobs are logical entities in `state.mobs[]`, visuals are separate.** Arrows must follow the same pattern: projectile state in engine, rendering via a new `ProjectileVisuals` in the renderer.

2. **No per-entity type system.** MobState is a single record per mob. Arrows would need:
   - Either: a new `ArrowState` type + `state.arrows: ArrowState[]`.
   - Or: a generic `ProjectileState` union type supporting arrows (and future fireballs, etc.).

3. **Event system is a FIFO queue, drained once per frame.** Useful for arrow→block collision audio. But no per-object callback system—projectiles can't emit events mid-tick; they queue events and the shell drains them after `step()`.

4. **Renderer syncs once per frame; no per-object on-demand renders.** Arrow models must be created/destroyed in `sync()`, not on spawn.

5. **All state is serializable (save/load).** Projectiles should either:
   - Not be saved (despawn on reload, like mobs do not).
   - Be added to SaveData, with serialization logic in blockChanges.ts.
   - **Safer: don't save.** Arrows are transient; they naturally despawn on load.

---

### EFFORT ESTIMATE FOR RANGED COMBAT + BOSS

**Projectile system (arrows):**
- Add `ArrowState` type to state.ts: ~15 lines.
- New `systems/projectiles.ts` (tick, collision detection, removal): ~100 lines (raycast per arrow, O(n) mobs per arrow, call `tryHitMob`).
- New `systems/archery.ts` (fire arrow on right-click or secondary attack): ~40 lines (spawn arrow from skeleton or player with aim + velocity).
- Extend `combat.ts` → add `tryHitArrow()` helper or reuse existing damage logic.
- Renderer: `createArrowVisuals()` in render/: ~60 lines (simple line/cylinder mesh, or extruded sprite).
- Events: add `"arrowFired"` + `"arrowHit"` to GameEvent union: ~5 lines.
- Tests: basic arrow trajectory, mob hit, despawn: ~80 lines.
- Recipes for bow + arrows: ~3 recipes in recipes.ts.
- Item defs for bow + arrow: ~2 entries in items.ts.

**Skeleton AI upgrade (shoot arrows):**
- Modify mobAI.ts tickMobs: detect if skeleton within range + LOS → fire arrow instead of melee, reset attackTimer. ~20 lines (conditional branch).
- Or: add a separate `tickSkeletonRangedAttack()` post-tickMobs for skeleton-specific logic. ~30 lines.
- Skeleton becomes a threat without closing to melee distance; difficulty increases.

**Boss encounter (new mob kind):**
- Add `"boss_warden"` (or theme of choice) to MobKind union (types.ts).
- New MOB_TEMPLATE in mobs.ts with high hp (50+), detectRange (20), custom attack patterns.
- Boss model: reuse `createMobModel()` with scale factor + custom colors, or add `createBossModel()` with more geometry (~100 lines).
- Boss AI variant: multi-phase behavior (aggressive melee → ranged → summon minions). Post-tickMobs logic in a new `tickBossAI()` system. ~150 lines.
- Boss loot table in mobLoot.ts (rare drops, e.g., boss_egg, boss_trophy). ~20 lines.
- Boss spawn: manual spawn command or rare hostile spawn condition (e.g., after killing N hostile mobs). ~30 lines.
- Boss encounter room: optional, requires special block structure or dungeon generation. Can skip for MVP.

**Total new code (arrows + skeleton archery + simple boss):**
- Engine systems: ~250 lines (projectiles + archery + boss AI).
- Renderer: ~100 lines (arrow visuals + boss model).
- Types/config: ~40 lines.
- Tests: ~150 lines.
- Recipes/items: ~20 lines.

**Estimate: 550–700 lines of new code, ~3–5 days solo (1–2 days if projectile logic is simplified).**

**Critical unknowns:**
- Does the team want arrows to stick in terrain, or disappear on block hit?
- Boss loot: unique items, or rare crafting materials?
- Boss AI: melee + ranged, or pure ranged (harder to balance)?
- How many skeletons should spawn with archery enabled?

---

---

### FEASIBILITY VERDICT

**Yes, ranged combat (arrows + skeleton archery) is feasible with moderate effort.** The engine is well-structured for new projectile mechanics:
- Event system is flexible (add arrowFired/arrowHit events).
- Mob AI is modular (add ranged attack logic to skeleton branch).
- Renderer follows the same create/sync/dispose pattern (arrow visuals follow mob visuals).
- No physics engine needed; simple raycast-per-frame collision suffices.

**Boss encounter is also feasible.** Bosses are just mobs with higher HP, custom AI, and larger geometry. The existing spawn/removal/loot system scales. Multi-phase AI is doable post-tickMobs.

**Lowest-friction path:** Arrows as transient entities, skeletons shoot on a simple timer, boss is a single large skeletal mob with high HP and a multi-part attack pattern.

> AGENT

I now have a grounded picture of all three directions. Here's the synthesis from exploring the actual code:

## What I found

**① Caves & survival tension (block-light + lava + oxygen)** — *Largest, riskiest.*
The mesher (`lib/world/meshing.ts`) shades faces from normal direction + AO only; there's **no per-voxel light channel**. Day/night is a single global scalar (`dayNight.ts`) driving one directional + hemisphere light, so caves are lit identically to the surface. Adding real light means a propagation engine (BFS, 0–15 decay, two light types), a new per-voxel storage channel (a parallel `Uint8Array` = ~39 MB, or risky bit-packing that touches the save-critical voxel index), and per-vertex light sampling in the mesher hot path. Est. ~1,500–2,200 LoC, multi-week, with real performance/save-format risk.

**② Exploration & loot (chests + dungeons)** — *Medium, foundational.*
Chests reuse what already exists cleanly: the immutable slot algebra (`inventory.ts`), the right-click interact precedence (`interact.ts`, where bed/furnace already register), and the furnace's "open a panel" state pattern. The one real blocker is that **no per-block metadata system exists** — blocks are bare `BlockId`s — so a chest's contents need a new `blockEntities` field in `SaveData` and an additive save bump to v4 (well-precedented; v3 was additive). Chests-only needs **no worldgen change**. Dungeons are a fast-follow that hooks the existing `placeStructures` pass but would bump the worldgen hash (SAVE_KEY bump + re-baseline).

**③ Endgame & ranged combat (bows/arrows + boss)** — *Smallest, lowest-risk, most self-contained.*
A transient `projectiles[]` array (not saved), a skeleton archery branch in `mobAI.ts`, and a boss as a scaled-up mob reusing the model builder + loot table. **No save-format or worldgen impact.** Est. ~550–700 LoC, a few days. Gives the diamond-tier grind an actual goal.

## My recommendation

I'd build **② persistent storage (chests) first**, scoped to chests + a reusable block-entity system (dungeons as a fast-follow, not in the same slice). Reasoning: it's the most-felt gap — right now *everything* you own rides in 36 slots on your person, with nowhere to stash anything — and it's foundational. The block-entity metadata layer it introduces is what later makes dungeons' loot, and a boss's reward, have somewhere to live. It's medium effort with heavy reuse, and an additive save bump rather than a worldgen-breaking one.

That said, this is a genuine product-direction call, so let me confirm before I design the detailed plan.

1	import { HOTBAR_SLOTS, INVENTORY_SLOTS, MAX_HEARTS, MAX_HUNGER, MAX_STACK_SIZE } from "@/lib/game/config";
2	import { ARMOR_SLOTS, createEmptyArmorEquipment, createEmptySlot, createSlot, ITEM_DEF_BY_ID } from "@/lib/game/items";
3	import type { EquippedArmor, SaveData, SaveDataV1, SaveDataV2, InventorySlot } from "@/lib/game/types";
4	
5	/**
6	 * Migrates a v1 save (40 slots, 10-slot hotbar) to v2 (36 slots, 9-slot
7	 * hotbar): non-empty slots are packed in order, stackable items merge into
8	 * earlier stacks, and anything that still overflows 36 slots is dropped.
9	 */
10	/** Coerces a persisted hotbar index to a finite integer within 0..HOTBAR_SLOTS-1. */
11	function normalizeSelectedSlot(value: number): number {
12	  const index = Number.isFinite(value) ? Math.floor(value) : 0;
13	  return Math.max(0, Math.min(HOTBAR_SLOTS - 1, index));
14	}
15	
16	export function migrateSaveV1toV2(save: SaveDataV1): SaveDataV2 {
17	  const migrated: SaveDataV2 = { ...save, version: 2, selectedSlot: normalizeSelectedSlot(save.selectedSlot) };
18	
19	  if (Array.isArray(save.inventorySlots)) {
20	    const packed: Array<{ id: string | null; count: number; durability?: number }> = [];
21	    for (const saved of save.inventorySlots) {
22	      if (!saved?.id || saved.count <= 0) continue;
23	      // Items without durability stack; merge them into an earlier stack first.
24	      const stackable = ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[saved.id] ? !ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[saved.id].maxDurability : false;
25	      let remaining = saved.count;
26	      if (stackable) {
27	        for (const slot of packed) {
28	          if (slot.id !== saved.id || slot.count >= MAX_STACK_SIZE) continue;
29	          const moved = Math.min(MAX_STACK_SIZE - slot.count, remaining);
30	          slot.count += moved;
31	          remaining -= moved;
32	          if (remaining === 0) break;
33	        }
34	      }
35	      if (remaining > 0 && packed.length < INVENTORY_SLOTS) {
36	        packed.push({ id: saved.id, count: remaining, durability: saved.durability });
37	      }
38	    }
39	    migrated.inventorySlots = packed;
40	  }
41	
42	  return migrated;
43	}
44	
45	/**
46	 * Migrates a v2 save to v3 — a pure version bump. The new persisted fields
47	 * (dayClock, hearts, hunger, spawnPoint) are optional, so an older save simply
48	 * loads with the engine's defaults for them.
49	 */
50	export function migrateSaveV2toV3(save: SaveDataV2): SaveData {
51	  return { ...save, version: 3 };
52	}
53	
54	// Storage is injectable so save logic can be tested without a browser.
55	export function readSave(saveKey: string, storage: Storage = localStorage): SaveData | null {
56	  try {
57	    const raw = storage.getItem(saveKey);
58	    if (!raw) return null;
59	    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as SaveData | SaveDataV2 | SaveDataV1;
60	    if (!parsed || !Number.isFinite(parsed.seed) || !Array.isArray(parsed.changes)) return null;
61	    let migrated: SaveDataV2 | SaveData = parsed.version === 1 ? migrateSaveV1toV2(parsed) : parsed;
62	    if (migrated.version === 2) migrated = migrateSaveV2toV3(migrated);
63	    if (migrated.version !== 3) return null;
64	    return migrated;
65	  } catch {
66	    return null;
67	  }
68	}
69	
70	export function writeSave(saveKey: string, data: SaveData, storage: Storage = localStorage): void {
71	  storage.setItem(saveKey, JSON.stringify(data));
72	}
73	
74	export function inventorySlotsSnapshot(inventory: InventorySlot[]): Array<{ id: string | null; count: number; durability?: number }> {
75	  return inventory.map((slot) => ({ id: slot.id, count: slot.count, durability: slot.durability }));
76	}
77	
78	/**
79	 * Rebuilds inventory slots from a save, dropping unknown items, clamping
80	 * counts and durability, and skipping broken gear. Supports both the current
81	 * inventorySlots shape and the legacy inventoryCounts shape. Returns null when
82	 * the save carries no inventory.
83	 */
84	export function restoreInventorySlots(save: SaveData): InventorySlot[] | null {
85	  if (Array.isArray(save.inventorySlots)) {
86	    const slots = Array.from({ length: INVENTORY_SLOTS }, () => createEmptySlot());
87	    for (let i = 0; i < Math.min(INVENTORY_SLOTS, save.inventorySlots.length); i += 1) {
88	      const saved = save.inventorySlots[i];
89	      if (!saved?.id || saved.count <= 0) continue;
90	      if (!ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[saved.id]) continue;
91	      const slot = createSlot(saved.id, Math.min(MAX_STACK_SIZE, Math.max(0, Math.floor(saved.count))));
92	      if ((slot.kind === "tool" || slot.kind === "weapon" || slot.kind === "armor") && slot.maxDurability) {
93	        if (typeof saved.durability === "number") {
94	          const loadedDurability = Math.floor(saved.durability);
95	          if (loadedDurability <= 0) continue;
96	          slot.durability = Math.max(1, Math.min(slot.maxDurability, loadedDurability));
97	        } else {
98	          slot.durability = slot.maxDurability;
99	        }
100	      }
101	      slots[i] = slot;
102	    }
103	    return slots;
104	  }
105	
106	  if (save.inventoryCounts) {
107	    const slots = Array.from({ length: INVENTORY_SLOTS }, () => createEmptySlot());
108	    let cursor = 0;
109	    for (const [id, raw] of Object.entries(save.inventoryCounts)) {
110	      if (!ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[id]) continue;
111	      let remaining = Math.max(0, Math.floor(raw));
112	      while (remaining > 0 && cursor < slots.length) {
113	        const add = Math.min(MAX_STACK_SIZE, remaining);
114	        slots[cursor] = createSlot(id, add);
115	        cursor += 1;
116	        remaining -= add;
117	      }
118	    }
119	    return slots;
120	  }
121	
122	  return null;
123	}
124	
125	/** Restores equipped armor from a save, ignoring ids that are not valid armor for the slot. */
126	export function restoreEquippedArmor(save: SaveData): EquippedArmor | null {
127	  if (!save.equippedArmor) return null;
128	  const next = createEmptyArmorEquipment();
129	  for (const armorSlot of ARMOR_SLOTS) {
130	    const equippedId = save.equippedArmor[armorSlot];
131	    if (!equippedId) continue;
132	    const def = ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[equippedId];
133	    if (def?.kind !== "armor" || def.armorSlot !== armorSlot) continue;
134	    next[armorSlot] = equippedId;
135	  }
136	  return next;
137	}
138	
139	export function restoreSelectedSlot(save: SaveData): number | null {
140	  if (typeof save.selectedSlot !== "number") return null;
141	  return normalizeSelectedSlot(save.selectedSlot);
142	}
143	
144	/** Restores the day clock from a save (finite, non-negative); null if absent/invalid. */
145	export function restoreDayClock(save: SaveData): number | null {
146	  if (typeof save.dayClock !== "number" || !Number.isFinite(save.dayClock) || save.dayClock < 0) return null;
147	  return save.dayClock;
148	}
149	
150	/** Restores hearts clamped to 1..MAX_HEARTS; null if absent/invalid. */
151	export function restoreHearts(save: SaveData): number | null {
152	  if (typeof save.hearts !== "number" || !Number.isFinite(save.hearts)) return null;
153	  return Math.max(1, Math.min(MAX_HEARTS, Math.floor(save.hearts)));
154	}
155	
156	/** Restores hunger clamped to 0..MAX_HUNGER; null if absent/invalid. */
157	export function restoreHungerLevel(save: SaveData): number | null {
158	  if (typeof save.hunger !== "number" || !Number.isFinite(save.hunger)) return null;
159	  return Math.max(0, Math.min(MAX_HUNGER, Math.floor(save.hunger)));
160	}
161	
162	/** Restores the bed respawn point; null if absent or explicitly cleared. */
163	export function restoreSpawnPoint(save: SaveData): { x: number; y: number; z: number } | null {
164	  const sp = save.spawnPoint;
165	  if (!sp || !Number.isFinite(sp.x) || !Number.isFinite(sp.y) || !Number.isFinite(sp.z)) return null;
166	  return { x: Math.floor(sp.x), y: Math.floor(sp.y), z: Math.floor(sp.z) };
167	}
168	
```

1	import * as THREE from "three";
2	import { BlockId, voxelRaycast } from "@/lib/world";
3	import { BREED_FED_WINDOW_SECONDS, EYE_HEIGHT, MINE_REACH, SLEEP_ALLOWED_BELOW_DAYLIGHT, SLEEP_FADE_SECONDS, SLEEP_HOSTILE_RADIUS } from "@/lib/game/config";
4	import { adjustSlotCount, consumeToolDurability } from "@/lib/game/inventory";
5	import type { MobKind } from "@/lib/game/types";
6	import type { EmitGameEvent, GameState } from "../state";
7	import { findAimedMobIndex } from "./combat";
8	import { lookDirection } from "./playerMotion";
9	
10	const scratchEye = new THREE.Vector3();
11	const scratchDir = new THREE.Vector3();
12	
13	/** Blocks whose right-click runs a handler instead of placing the held block. */
14	export type InteractiveKind = "bed" | "furnace";
15	
16	export const INTERACTIVE_BLOCKS: Partial<Record<BlockId, InteractiveKind>> = {
17	  [BlockId.Bed]: "bed",
18	  [BlockId.Furnace]: "furnace"
19	};
20	
21	/**
22	 * Right-click "use" on the aimed block. Returns true when the click was an
23	 * interaction (consumed — the caller must NOT then place a block), false when
24	 * the aimed block has no behavior and placement should proceed.
25	 *
26	 * This is the shared hook future interactive blocks (furnace, …) plug into:
27	 * add a `BlockId → kind` entry and a branch below.
28	 */
29	export function tryInteractBlock(state: GameState, emit: EmitGameEvent): boolean {
30	  const { world, player } = state;
31	  scratchEye.set(player.position.x, player.position.y + EYE_HEIGHT, player.position.z);
32	  lookDirection(player.yaw, player.pitch, scratchDir);
33	  const result = voxelRaycast(world, scratchEye, scratchDir, MINE_REACH);
34	  if (!result) return false;
35	
36	  const block = world.get(result.hit.x, result.hit.y, result.hit.z) as BlockId;
37	  const kind = INTERACTIVE_BLOCKS[block];
38	  if (!kind) return false;
39	
40	  if (kind === "bed") return interactBed(state, emit, result.hit.x, result.hit.y, result.hit.z);
41	  if (kind === "furnace") return interactFurnace(state, emit);
42	  return false;
43	}
44	
45	/** What each breedable animal is fed to enter "in love" mode. */
46	const FEED_ITEMS: Partial<Record<MobKind, string>> = {
47	  sheep: "wheat",
48	  horse: "wheat",
49	  chicken: "seeds"
50	};
51	
52	/**
53	 * Right-click an adult animal with its feed item to put it "in love" (toward
54	 * breeding). First in the right-click precedence so feeding wins over placing or
55	 * tilling when an animal is in the crosshair. Returns true when an animal was fed.
56	 */
57	export function tryFeedAimedMob(state: GameState, emit: EmitGameEvent): boolean {
58	  const slot = state.inventory[state.selectedSlot];
59	  if (!slot?.id || slot.count <= 0) return false;
60	  const index = findAimedMobIndex(state);
61	  if (index < 0) return false;
62	  const mob = state.mobs[index];
63	  if (mob.hostile || FEED_ITEMS[mob.kind] !== slot.id) return false;
64	  if (mob.ageTimer > 0 || mob.fedTimer > 0) return false; // babies and already-in-love animals decline
65	
66	  state.inventory = adjustSlotCount(state.inventory, slot.id, -1, state.selectedSlot) ?? state.inventory;
67	  mob.fedTimer = BREED_FED_WINDOW_SECONDS;
68	  emit({ type: "mobFed", kind: mob.kind });
69	  return true;
70	}
71	
72	/** Opens the inventory in furnace mode so its smelting recipes unlock. */
73	function interactFurnace(state: GameState, emit: EmitGameEvent): boolean {
74	  state.inventoryOpen = true;
75	  state.craftingStation = "furnace";
76	  emit({ type: "openedStation", station: "furnace" });
77	  return true;
78	}
79	
80	/** Sleep in a bed: only at night, only when no hostile is near. Sets the respawn point. */
81	function interactBed(state: GameState, emit: EmitGameEvent, x: number, y: number, z: number): boolean {
82	  if (state.daylight >= SLEEP_ALLOWED_BELOW_DAYLIGHT) {
83	    emit({ type: "sleepDenied", reason: "daylight" });
84	    return true;
85	  }
86	  for (const mob of state.mobs) {
87	    if (mob.hostile && mob.position.distanceTo(state.player.position) <= SLEEP_HOSTILE_RADIUS) {
88	      emit({ type: "sleepDenied", reason: "hostiles" });
89	      return true;
90	    }
91	  }
92	
93	  state.spawnPoint = { x, y, z };
94	  state.sleepTimer = SLEEP_FADE_SECONDS;
95	  emit({ type: "sleepStarted" });
96	  return true;
97	}
98	
99	/**
100	 * Right-click "use" of the held item on the aimed block: a hoe tills grass/dirt
101	 * into farmland, seeds plant wheat on farmland. Returns true when an action
102	 * happened (consumes the click), false to fall through to block placement.
103	 */
104	export function tryUseHeldItem(state: GameState, emit: EmitGameEvent, rng: () => number): boolean {
105	  void rng; // reserved for future randomized uses (bone meal, …)
106	  const slot = state.inventory[state.selectedSlot];
107	  if (!slot?.id || slot.count <= 0) return false;
108	  const isHoe = slot.id.endsWith("_hoe");
109	  const isSeeds = slot.id === "seeds";
110	  if (!isHoe && !isSeeds) return false;
111	
112	  const { world, player } = state;
113	  scratchEye.set(player.position.x, player.position.y + EYE_HEIGHT, player.position.z);
114	  lookDirection(player.yaw, player.pitch, scratchDir);
115	  const result = voxelRaycast(world, scratchEye, scratchDir, MINE_REACH);
116	  if (!result) return false;
117	  const { x, y, z } = result.hit;
118	  const block = world.get(x, y, z) as BlockId;
119	
120	  if (isHoe) {
121	    if (block !== BlockId.Grass && block !== BlockId.Dirt) return false;
122	    state.blockChanges.set(x, y, z, BlockId.Farmland);
123	    state.inventory = consumeToolDurability(state.inventory, state.selectedSlot, 1) ?? state.inventory;
124	    state.worldMeshDirty = true;
125	    emit({ type: "tilledSoil" });
126	    return true;
127	  }
128	
129	  // Seeds: plant on farmland when the cell above is clear.
130	  if (block !== BlockId.Farmland || world.get(x, y + 1, z) !== BlockId.Air) return false;
131	  state.blockChanges.set(x, y + 1, z, BlockId.WheatStage0);
132	  state.inventory = adjustSlotCount(state.inventory, slot.id, -1, state.selectedSlot) ?? state.inventory;
133	  state.worldMeshDirty = true;
134	  emit({ type: "plantedSeed" });
135	  return true;
136	}
137

1	import { BlockId, VoxelWorld } from "@/lib/world";
2	
3	/**
4	 * Tracks player edits to the generated world as deltas against the worldgen
5	 * baseline — the heart of the save format. A block restored to its original
6	 * value drops out of the delta set, keeping saves minimal.
7	 */
8	export type BlockChangeTracker = {
9	  /** Tracked write: updates the world and the delta set. */
10	  set(x: number, y: number, z: number, block: BlockId): void;
11	  /** Replays saved deltas onto a freshly generated world (boot only). */
12	  applySavedChanges(changes: Array<[number, number]>): void;
13	  /** Current deltas as [voxelIndex, blockId] pairs, ready to persist. */
14	  changes(): Array<[number, number]>;
15	};
16	
17	export function createBlockChangeTracker(world: VoxelWorld): BlockChangeTracker {
18	  const changedBlocks = new Map<number, number>();
19	  const baselineByIndex = new Map<number, number>();
20	
21	  return {
22	    set(x, y, z, block) {
23	      if (!world.inBounds(x, y, z)) return;
24	      const idx = world.index(x, y, z);
25	      if (!baselineByIndex.has(idx)) baselineByIndex.set(idx, world.get(x, y, z));
26	      world.set(x, y, z, block);
27	      const baseline = baselineByIndex.get(idx) ?? BlockId.Air;
28	      if (block === baseline) changedBlocks.delete(idx);
29	      else changedBlocks.set(idx, block);
30	    },
31	
32	    applySavedChanges(changes) {
33	      const layer = world.sizeX * world.sizeZ;
34	      for (const [idx, block] of changes) {
35	        const y = Math.floor(idx / layer);
36	        const rem = idx - y * layer;
37	        const z = Math.floor(rem / world.sizeX);
38	        const x = rem - z * world.sizeX;
39	        if (!world.inBounds(x, y, z)) continue;
40	        world.set(x, y, z, block as BlockId);
41	        changedBlocks.set(idx, block);
42	      }
43	    },
44	
45	    changes() {
46	      return [...changedBlocks.entries()];
47	    }
48	  };
49	}
50	
```
